### 6.91 Stand-ins of the seasonal swing (GEN.1039)

`physics/seasonal_swing.py` gives the temperature through the year at a latitude from the stored tilt and the orbit: the day's mean light through an eccentric orbit, a local energy balance about the annual-mean state with the heat capacity of the sea's mixed layer, the ground's skin and the air column, and the damping by the heat the air carries (the share the means already use). It returns the Earth's tropics within 3 K and its 60 degree zonal mean (ocean share 0.4) at 17 K peak to peak against about 25 K observed, a dry Mars within 8 percent of the fourth-root radiative response, and a polar summer hotter than the equator above a tilt of 54.8 degrees. The first harmonic of a small tilt matches the closed-form response to 0.3 percent. Not read by the generator until GEN.1048 (train 12). Draft: `docs/design/systems/weird-worlds.md` W1.

### 6.92 Stand-ins of the resonant chains (GEN.1043)

`physics/resonant_chains.py` finds the chains of three or more planets whose neighbouring periods are near 2:1, 3:2, 4:3 or 5:3, and gives the eccentricity each neighbour forces on a planet near a first-order resonance (`e_inner = mu' alpha |f_27| / ((p - 1) |Delta|)`, `e_outer = mu |f_31| / (p |Delta|)`, the Laplace-coefficient resonant terms of the disturbing function, Murray and Dermott 1999 and Lithwick, Xie and Wu 2012, recalled and derived), held at the width of the resonance and short of crossing orbits. It finds TRAPPIST-1 as one chain of seven, Kepler-223 as a chain of four, the Galilean moons as a chain of three and the Solar System as none; Io comes out at 0.0052 against the observed forced 0.0041 and Europa at 0.0083 against 0.0094; TRAPPIST-1's d to h come out between 0.003 and 0.007, the design note's thousandths. Not read by the generator until GEN.1049.

### 6.93 Stand-ins of the collisional mantle stripping (GEN.1040)

`physics/mantle_stripping.py` is oddity W10: a head-on giant impact that blows off the rock and leaves an iron-rich planet (Mercury about 70 percent iron by mass, Kepler-107 c about 70 percent, an Earth-like planet a third). It is the law of Marcus, Stewart, Sasselov and Hernquist 2010 (ApJL 712, L73; arXiv:0907.0234, read) from the masses and the speed to the largest remnant: `x = Q_R / Q*_RD`, `M_lr / M_tot = 1 - x/2` (equation 2), the remnant's iron `0.33 + 0.25 x^1.65` (equation 3), `Q*_RD = 1e-4 R_C1^1.2 V^0.8` erg/g (equation 4). The oddity's rate is the registry row WR4 of 6.82. Checked against the one giant-impact run with every number printed (Bonomo and others 2019, Fig. 3), Kepler-107 b and c through the repository's mass-radius model, and Mercury, in `src/tests/test_mantle_stripping.py`. The module is not read by the generator until GEN.1048 wires W10 (and GEN.1047 the table).

### 6.94 Stand-ins of the irregular zone (GEN.1076)

`physics/irregular_zone.py` gives where a captured small body can stay round a planet: the Hill radius (Jewitt and Haghighipour 2007 equation 1, 0.35, 0.43, 0.47 and 0.77 AU for the four giants), the time-averaged stable limits of a prograde and a retrograde orbit as shares of it, the Kozai band (`moon_composition.KOZAI_FORBIDDEN_DEG`) and the inner edge set by the outermost regular moon (Vokrouhlicky, Nesvorny and Levison 2008). Design `docs/design/systems/captured-asteroid-moons.md` 2.1 and 4.3. The module is not read by the generator until the captured-moon wiring.

| IZ2 | P | T1 | L0 | irregular_zone.RETROGRADE_STABLE_FRACTION 0.55, the share of the Hill radius out to which a retrograde orbit is stable | 0.5 to 0.6 | retrograde irregulars of the four giants | a stable retrograde body found beyond 0.6 of the Hill radius | the same integration-fitted limit | Vokrouhlicky, Nesvorny and Levison 2008, quoting Nesvorny et al. 2003: 0.5 to 0.6 depending on the mean eccentricity; 0.55 is the middle |

### 6.99 Stand-ins of the belt density factor (GEN.1093)

`physics/belt_density.py` gives a belt's objects per square AU above a stated diameter (1, 10 and 100 km on the information page) and its mass per square AU, from the belt's annulus, its stored mass, a broken power size law and the bodies the ledger keeps one by one: the tail is normalised to the mass the listed bodies leave, so the listed bodies and the tail add to the belt's mass exactly. The main belt law is Ivezic et al. 2001 read as differential exponents (2.3 below 5 km, 4 above, so cumulative 1.3 and 3.0); read as cumulative it leaves 3 asteroids above 40 km, the differential reading about 130 (the belt has some hundreds). With the nine biggest asteroids listed the Solar System's belt comes out at about 1.4 million objects above 1 km (Ivezic: 530,000) and about 120 above 100 km (some 200). Not read by the generator until UX.240.

### 6.96 Stand-ins of the irregular orbits (GEN.1078)

`physics/irregular_orbits.py` draws the orbits of captured small bodies before the irregular zone (6.94) cuts them: the size of the orbit over the Hill radius, its eccentricity, its inclination, and the fragments of a family. Design `docs/design/systems/captured-asteroid-moons.md` 2.1, 2.4 and 4.3. The module is not read by the generator until the captured-moon wiring. By stability alone the survivors split 4.5 retrograde to 1 prograde at a Jupiter-like planet (the known irregulars: 88 to 19) and about 1 to 1 at the far giants, so the excess retrograde moons of Uranus (8 to 1) are not modelled.

### 6.97 Stand-ins of the belt origins (GEN.1088)

`physics/belt_origin.py` gives every belt the cause that made it (design `belt-origins.md` sections 3 to 5): a gap in the planet chain, its unfinished tail, a pile-up at the snow line, the outer disc, the remains of a destroyed planet or rubble of a collision, and the two things that take mass afterwards, a giant's chaotic zone and resonance gaps and a giant that moved through. It stores the cause, the mass shares and the sentence. The values below are the ones the module holds; the numbers read in papers are the rows BR2 and BR4 of 6.95, which this table points to rather than repeats. The module is not read by the generator until GEN.1068 and GEN.1092.

### 6.98 Stand-ins of the band winds (GEN.1110)

`physics/band_winds.py` estimates the mean surface wind by day and by night in each latitude band as an order of magnitude: the heat-engine speed `sqrt((dT/T) R_s dT)` of the day-night and equator-to-pole differences, the thermal wind `(R_s/f)(dT_ep/L) ln(P_s/P_top)` where the world turns fast, their smooth minimum, a share of it reaching the ground (less at night), the diurnal flow cut at high latitude by the inertial limit, the Hadley edge `sqrt(5/3 Ro_T)` to name the regime, and a fade below about 1 kPa. The Earth comes out at 4 to 12 m/s by day (mid-latitudes 5 to 7), Mars at 2 to 7, Venus under half a metre a second and Titan about 2. Not read by the generator until the seven-band climate (GEN.1108) feeds it.

| fill_id | kind | tier | tweak | constant | knobs | validity | falsifier | replaced_by | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BW1 | P | T9 | L2 | band_winds.PRESSURE_FADE_PA 1000 Pa, where the surface wind is half what the air aloft allows | 300 to 3000 | air under a few kilopascals | a measured surface wind on a thin-air world (Mars, Pluto, Triton) that the fade misses by more than a factor of three | a drag law from the air's density and the surface roughness | Boss's "about 1 kPa" in the item (GEN.1110) |
| BW2 | P | T9 | L3 | band_winds.PRESSURE_FADE_EXPONENT 2, how sharply the fade falls below its pressure | 1 to 4 | the same | Mars (600 Pa) and Pluto (1 Pa) winds both measured | the same drag law | chosen so Mars keeps a quarter of the wind aloft and Pluto nothing |
| BW3 | P | T6 | L1 | band_winds.THERMAL_WIND_LOG_PRESSURE 1.6, `ln(P_s / P_top)` of the thermal wind | 1.0 to 2.3 | worlds with a tropopause | a world whose tropopause is far from a fifth of the surface pressure (Venus, Titan) | the tropopause pressure of the radiative-convective solve | the Earth's tropopause is 0.2 of the surface pressure, recalled |
| BW4 | P | T9 | L3 | band_winds.SURFACE_COUPLING 0.3, the share of the wind aloft that reaches the surface by day | 0.15 to 0.5 | rocky worlds with air | surface winds off the Earth's 5 to 8 m/s in the mid-latitudes | a boundary-layer drag law on the surface roughness | tuned to the Earth's mid-latitude surface wind |
| BW5 | P | T9 | L3 | band_winds.NIGHT_COUPLING 0.5, the share of the day's coupling left at night | 0.3 to 0.8 | worlds with a stable night layer | a measured night to day wind ratio far from a half | a boundary-layer stability model | the Earth's night winds are about half the day's, recalled |
| BW6 | P | T6 | L0 | band_winds.TROPICS_LIMIT_DEG 60, the latitude where westerlies give way to polar winds | 50 to 70 | worlds with a Hadley cell | a general-circulation run with the Ferrel cell ending far from 60 degrees | the Ferrel cell's edge from the same scaling | the Earth's polar front is near 60 degrees, recalled |

### 6.89 Stand-ins of the rogue planet drift (GEN.1114)

`physics/rogue_drift.py` gives where an ejected planet is relative to its star a time after the kick, by the exact solution of Hill's equations in the star's rotating frame (`x = (2 Omega v / kappa^2)(1 - cos kappa t) + (u / kappa) sin kappa t`, the slide along the orbit at `4 Omega A / kappa^2` times the along-orbit kick, the vertical oscillation at `nu`), with the velocity relative to the star and a ring form that turns the along-orbit offset into a wrapped azimuth. It agrees with a direct nonlinear orbit integration in a flat-rotation-curve potential to within a few percent of the separation out to 1 kpc and 200 Myr, and gives the design note's medians (about 30 pc after 10 Myr, 270 pc after 100 Myr for a 3 km/s isotropic kick). The kick is an input. Not read by the generator until the virtual-donor placement (GEN.1111).

| fill_id | kind | tier | tweak | constant | knobs | validity | falsifier | replaced_by | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| GD1 | P | T1 | L0 | rogue_drift.OORT_A_KM_S_KPC 15.3 km/s/kpc, the shear at the Sun | 14.8 to 15.8 | the Sun's neighbourhood | an Oort constant measured at another galactocentric radius | the potential's own `Omega` and `kappa` at the sector's radius (`Galaxy.from_frequencies`) | Bovy 2017, quoted in the drift note, section 3.1 |
| GD2 | P | T1 | L0 | rogue_drift.OORT_B_KM_S_KPC -11.9 km/s/kpc | -12.4 to -11.4 | the Sun's neighbourhood | the same | the same | Bovy 2017, quoted in the drift note |

### 6.88 Stand-ins of the rogue planet age (GEN.1112, RP5)

`physics/rogue_age.py` gives a rogue planet's age (the ejecting star's, drawn from the galaxy's star-formation `AgeLaw`), the delay after formation at which it was thrown out (power-law density in time from the shortest delay to the parent's age, an even share of each decade at index 1) and the time since ejection, the three numbers the heat law, the drift and the history sentence need. The ejection record of GEN.1119 replaces the drawn delay with the recorded one (`from_record`). Not read by the generator until the rogue rows are created (GEN.1111).

| fill_id | kind | tier | tweak | constant | knobs | validity | falsifier | replaced_by | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| RG1 | P | T8 | L2 | rogue_age.EJECTION_DELAY_MIN_GY 0.001 Gy, the earliest ejection after formation | 0.0003 to 0.01 | ejected planets and embryos | an ejection record (GEN.1119) with a large share of ejections earlier than 1 Myr after formation | the delay distribution of the architecture's own ejection records | the gas disc's first Myr is before planets scatter; an educated guess from physics |
| RG2 | P | T8 | L3 | rogue_age.DELAY_INDEX 1, the ejection delay's density goes as t^-1 | 0 to 2 | the scattering of giants and embryos | ejection records whose delays are not spread evenly over the decades of time | the delay distribution of the ejection records (GEN.1119) | Chatterjee et al. 2008 give scattering times from 1e6 to 1e9 yr and beyond, recalled; the even spread is ours |

### 6.87 Stand-ins of the rogue planet ejection speed (GEN.1112, RP6)

`physics/rogue_kick.py` gives the speed at infinity of a body ejected by a scattering planet, `v_inf = v_esc(a) sqrt(STEP (-ln(1 - u)) / 2)`, from the star's escape speed at the scatterer's distance and the fractional energy step of one encounter (`STEP = ENERGY_STEP_SCALE min(1, v_esc,p / v_orb)`, the first-passage overshoot of a random walk in orbital energy being exponential), the Safronov number cut for which scatterers eject, an isotropic default direction (the direction is held input, Boss's question open), and the rogue's velocity through the Galaxy as its host's plus the drift's relative velocity. A Jupiter scattering from 5.2 AU gives a median 6 km/s with a tail to 15; at 30 AU 2.5 km/s. Rogues of thin-disc hosts pass the Sun at a median of about 40 km/s, with 'Oumuamua's 26 and Borisov's 32 km/s inside the 10 to 90 percent window. Not read by the generator until the rogue rows (GEN.1111).

| fill_id | kind | tier | tweak | constant | knobs | validity | falsifier | replaced_by | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| RK1 | P | T8 | L3 | rogue_kick.ENERGY_STEP_SCALE 0.3, the fractional orbital-energy step of an encounter in units of min(1, v_esc,p / v_orb) | 0.15 to 1 | planets ejected by planets | ejection speeds from the architecture's own scattering records (GEN.1119, GEN.1113) whose median is not within a factor of two of the model's | the speeds read from the ejection records and from the N-body papers of GEN.1113 | tuned so giants at 5 to 30 AU give a median of 2.5 to 6 km/s, the drift note's 3 km/s and Sayed et al.'s 0.7 to 10.7 km/s |
| RK2 | P | T6 | L0 | rogue_kick.EJECTION_SAFRONOV_NUMBER 1, the Safronov number above which a scatterer ejects | 0.5 to 2 | scattering planets | a scatterer with Theta under 1 that ejects most of what it scatters in an N-body run | the scattering simulations read by GEN.1089 | Safronov 1972; Ford and Rasio 2008 use the same cut, recalled |

## 7. Gemini's first batch

### 7.1 Counts and recommendation

The paste of 10:52 AM EDT is about 34,000 characters. I count about 110 distinct factual claims (numbers, relations, attributions) and about 45 that can be checked against a paper reachable by us. I checked 20 against papers or computations already in hand: **8 agree, 7 are contradicted by a verified figure, 5 are unsupported, misattributed or internally inconsistent.** A full claim-by-claim check is its own job: I recommend one separate checking thread that works the other 25 checkable claims by opening the cited papers, and a second only if Boss wants the unverifiable theory content (the theory frontiers section, about 30 claims) screened for factual errors.

The paste is a copy of the document the thread received and sits unchanged at `/mnt/project-files/reports/gap-filling-plan-2026-10-17-files/gemini-batch-1-unverified.txt`.

### 7.2a Result of the checking thread (2026-10-17)

The checking thread finished at 11:49 AM EDT as it reported ([gemini-batch1-check.md](gemini-batch1-check.md)). It checked 51 claims (the 20 above plus 31 more): 10 agree, 17 partly, 16 contradicted by a read source, 4 unsupported, 4 misattributed. Five of the 20 above moved after more reading. The corrections that matter for the build items: the occupation centre is 10^9.33 Msun/h with width 0.21 dex (Gemini's 10^8.6 is the paper's no-reionization limit); the 1.9 low-mass stellar-to-halo slope is Garrison-Kimmel 2014 (1.92), not UniverseMachine; the halo formation redshift z50 is 1.02 at 1e10 and 0.74 at 1e12 (Gemini 1.9 and 1.2); the age-velocity normalisations are 21.1 and 39.4 km/s with indices 0.441 and 0.251 (Gemini 38.5, 62.0, 0.46, 0.42); the Courteau scatter is 0.097 dex about the paper's own line (79 markers digitised from its Fig 2; the 64 galaxies were a misreading). Requests now answered by a usable table: R2 (all three curves), R5, R12 (the z_re table with Busha's spread), R13 (Munoz ultra-faint sizes) and parts of R3, R6 and R10. Still open for Boss's list: R1, R4 beyond the Milky Way, R7, R8, R14 and parts of R6, R9, R10, R11. Three conflicts between two verified sources (thick-to-thin height ratio 2.5 Yoachim and Dalcanton against 4.2 Comerón 2018 median; thick-to-thin mass ratio against Vc; cluster system radius about 3.5 against about 6 times the galaxy's) are Decided, widen and keep both (Boss's cards, tapped 12:02 PM EDT (read from the session log and relayed in words by the coordinator)): the height ratio is drawn from 2.5 to 4.2, the mass ratio against Vc is widened between the two trends, the system radius ratio is drawn from about 3.5 to about 6, and every source stays in the record.

### 7.3 How results enter

Every Gemini figure is stored as a LEAD record next to the fill it concerns, with the claim number from 7.2, the verified figure it was checked against and a verdict. Agreeing claims raise the fill's evidence only if a verified source is also cited; contradicted claims are kept and show in the loader report as "LEAD rejected". The checking thread of 7.1 turns the remaining checkable claims into verdicts of the same shape; the second batch Boss expects uses the same intake. The theory frontier content (WDM, SIDM, fuzzy dark matter, thick-disc origin paradigms, assembly bias) is not a measurement and is never a default; it can only supply the form of a T-kind fill, for example the gas-rich turbulent collapse picture behind GR6, and it is labelled theory wherever it appears.

| X5 Galaxy priors | GR6, GX2, GX3, GX4 | X3 (GR6 uses the GR10 epoch); Boss items 1, 2 and 7 Decided | GEN.386, 402, 403 |

| X7 Body reading jobs | BC3, BC4 originals, BC7, BC9, BC10, BC12, BC13, BC19 | the originals wait for the files Boss fetches (item 5, 8.1); BC19 downloads at build time (6.5) | 9.0 |
| X8 Theory priors | BC5, BC6, BC8, BC14, BC17, BC18, AT.b4, AT.k7, AT.j, AT.m | X6 for the shared variables | 9.0 |

Which gaps block what, as far as I traced:

- **Block the 8.1 headline chain**: the two gaps named in GEN.301's own prerequisite line, GR4 (through GEN.447) and GR9c (through GEN.280). GR4 is derived and ready (X2). GR9c is a bracket with a virial-theorem part and one reading job (X4); it can ship as a labelled bracket without waiting for the review. I did not trace the other prerequisites of GEN.301 to gaps, so there may be more.
- **Block the 8.1 stamp** (the six-phase "Galaxy laws build" items listed in the lane report): all galaxy rows of 5.1, in the order X2, X3, X4, X5. Boss decided items 1, 2, 6 and 7 of section 8, and each has a fill that ships without further answers (item 6 also runs the quick VizieR search first).
- **Do not block 8.1**: every body and atmosphere row. They belong to 9.0, with the generation changes that alter stored values staying with 8.4 as already Decided; X6 is the exception worth pulling early because its outputs (the star regression and the water-share function) are inputs to GEN.472 phase 2 and the 8.1 back-scattered building blocks.
- **Epoch**: a fill that replaces a value later changes stored output and rides the pending epoch; the X1 registry records the epoch per fill so the builder of GEN.322-324 style rescatters can see which stand-ins sat inside it.

## 10. Limits of this report

- Nothing in the repository was changed. No papers were taken from refused publishers. Every number marked COMPUTED can be reproduced with the scripts in the files folder.
- READ means a thread opened the paper or table; abstracts only are marked "abstract READ". The Jordan 2005 and Rodriguez-Gomez 2016 statements rest on abstracts. Nothing in 7.2 is more than one check deep.
- The accreted-halo integral is a sketch: it applies the Moster z = 0 relation at the accretion mass, ignores satellite stripping, and over-predicts by a factor of 2 to 3 against Illustris. It is a shape source and a test, not a law.
- The oxygen-budget water share has no disk chemistry. The age-velocity rule has one closure target (the Milky Way) and so cannot yet be tested on a second galaxy.
- The Brewer 2016 regressions use the whole catalogue without its quality cut; the cut must be applied by the builder (4.7).
- Gemini claim counts (110 distinct, 45 checkable) are my estimates from reading the paste; the 20 checked are exactly those in 7.2.

Files: `/mnt/project-files/reports/gap-filling-plan-2026-10-17-files/` holds `water_share.py`, `acc.py`, `ws_stars.py`, `arx.py`, `brewer2016_vizier_J_ApJS_225_32.tsv` and `gemini-batch-1-unverified.txt`.

As built (GEN.498): `physics/oxygen_fugacity.py` fixes the one oxygen-fugacity scale, the Hirschmann 2021 iron-wustite buffer, and every stored delta-IW is on it (`mantle_delta_iw` was already read that way: Earth's upper mantle at IW+3.5 is QFM, which stands 3.1 to 3.7 above IW). Checked against the papers Boss supplied (9:55 PM to 10:17 PM EDT on 2026-10-22, from the coordinator's wake times): Hirschmann's Table 1 (every coefficient, sign and the iron-phase boundary), the NASA Glenn coefficients of CO, CO2, H2, H2O and O2, and Sossi et al. 2020 equation 4 are verified; the stand-ins that remain, each a row of `oxygen_fugacity.SOURCES`: the Frost 1991 FMQ and NNO constants (the text is paywalled; the form is confirmed by Anenburg 2026 and their gap at 1 GPa agrees with it, the absolute values are second hand from PetroBuffer), and the Sossi scale's 0.5 offset (derived from his gas ratios; his IW formula is in his Supplementary Materials, not read; Hirschmann's JANAF offset explains its size). Nothing stored changes.

As built (GEN.470, row BC12): `physics/saturation.py` and `physics/data/saturation_pressure.json` hold the one vapour-pressure function `saturation(species, T)`, in Pa with a status (`ok`, `cold-unverified` below 50 K, `below-fit` under the polynomial's own turning point, `above-triple`). It carries the Fray and Schmitt 2009 polynomials for Ne, N2, CO, Ar, O2, CH4, Kr, Xe, C2H6, C2H4, C2H2, H2S, CO2, NH3, SO2, HCN, CH3OH and C6H6 as quoted in the supplementary table of Lisse et al. (2ND-HAND; O2-3 and Ne-2 from part 01 section 7.2) and water by Feistel and Wagner (76.03 Pa at 250 K, 0.1626 Pa at 200 K, 611.657 Pa at the triple point). **Stand-ins, because the paper's per-fit windows are not read (BC12 stays open):** where a species has more than one fit the next takes over where the two cross (N2 36.1 K, CO 60.75 K, O2 23.15 and 43.79 K, CH3OH 157.55 K, Ne 13.26 K; each within 1 K of the known solid transition) or come closest (CO2 195.3 K, 1.1 percent apart); every `floor_k` is derived from the polynomial. CO2-1 serves the cold, not the CO2-2 that part 01 recommends from its triple-point error alone, because CO2-2 reaches 1e-9 bar at 64.7 K against the 86 K the latent heat of sublimation gives; H2S-1 alone, as part 01 does. **Known gaps:** at the triple point SO2 is 3.8 percent over the gas table's pressure, benzene 7 percent over and acetylene 3.6 percent under, every other species within 3 percent; H2 and He have no curve; above the triple point the liquid branch of the next paragraph takes over. GEN.497 replaces the cold end and the solid CO2 curve; GEN.452 makes the atmosphere read the function.

As built (GEN.497, row BC12, the cold end): `saturation` now serves five species from the laboratory fits `ln P[bar] = A - B/T + C ln T + D2 T + D3 T^2 + D4 T^3` (Lobo and Ferreira 2001) of Grundy et al. 2024 (N2, CO, CH4) and Blakley et al. 2024 (O2, NH3) instead of the Fray and Schmitt polynomial in the cold, and carbon dioxide from Span and Wagner 1996 equation 3.12 for the whole solid (842.65 Pa at 150 K, 103971 Pa at 195 K, 101325 Pa at 194.6855 K; the two Fray and Schmitt CO2 fits are gone). Each laboratory fit is used from the bottom of its fit range (N2 20 K, CO 25, CH4 30, O2 30, NH3 80) up to where it meets the polynomial (N2 35 K, the top of its range, with a published step to the alpha polynomial of a factor of 2.1; CO 54.4 K; CH4 51.0; O2 42.7; NH3 162.3); inside it the status is `ok`, under it the curve is Clausius-Clapeyron with the latent heat the fit has at its edge, flagged `below-fit`, and above it the polynomial keeps `cold-unverified` below 50 K. `tuning.COLD_END_LABORATORY_CORRECTION` (on) is the switch for the 2026 Grundy preprint, which multiplies all five by 1.19 +/- 0.03: on is its Table 3 A and B with the 2024 constants C to D4 (N2, CO and CH4: the reconstructions of part G4, max 0.6 percent), off is the 2024 set (N2, CO and CH4: the same curve divided by 1.19). Both sets and their hand-over temperatures are in the data file. **Stand-ins, labelled:** the fit range of each curve is the range of Grundy's data package and Blakley's data (finding 21 of the atmosphere composition research lists the narrower measured windows, N2 23 to 31 K, CO 26 to 35, CH4 31 to 43, O2 29 to 37, NH3 88 to 105); the Clausius-Clapeyron continuation below the range has no width stated (the "doubled in width" of the BC12 decision is not carried yet); the N2 step; the C to D4 of N2, CO and CH4 are reconstructions. BC12 (the original per-fit windows) stays open.

As built (GEN.452, the liquid branch of `saturation`): above the triple point and up to the critical point `saturation` returns the Wagner fits to the NIST saturation tables of part 01 section 6.2 (COMPUTED by the Atmosphere research lane, within 0.26 percent of the table), hydrogen cyanide the Perry DIPPR 101 correlation, and water the Wagner fit (3538.6 Pa at 300 K against the steam table's 3536.8). Above the critical temperature the status is `above-critical` and the pressure is infinite; acetylene has no liquid curve. Hydrogen is a Clausius-Clapeyron solid from its triple point with 1.02 kJ/mol (0.90 vapourisation from the gas table plus 0.12 fusion, RECALLED, a stand-in unchecked against Woolley 1948). The solid and liquid curves meet within the part-01 spread (N2 -2.7 percent, CH4 and H2S +1.7, NH3 +1.9, HCN +3.3, SO2 +3.8, benzene +7.1, others within 1 percent), so a step at the triple point is documented, not smoothed.

As built (GEN.452, the swap): `physics/atmosphere.py` no longer holds a vapour-pressure constant. `CONDENSATION_K` and the `h2s` copy line are gone and `vapour_pressure_kpa` reads `saturation.pressure_pa` (infinite above the critical temperature, so a hot world caps nothing), and `saturation.condensation_temperature_k(species, pressure)` gives the inverse (bisection, `None` above the critical pressure). The twelve other rows of the checked table of part 01 section 7.4 (H2, N2, CO, Ar, O2, CH4, SO2, H2O, H2S, NH3, C2H6, HCN at seven pressures from 100 kPa to 1 mPa) were reproduced to 0.12 K by the curves as they stood when the table was made; after the laboratory cold ends of GEN.497 and the Span and Wagner carbon dioxide curve the test allows 1 K above 60 K and 2 K below. **The thirteenth, CO2, departs from that table on purpose:** the table used CO2-2, which is 4 K cold at 100 Pa and 17 K cold at 1 mPa; the carbon dioxide curve is now the Span and Wagner sublimation equation (GEN.497). The cold gases (N2, CO, CH4) follow the laboratory cold ends, up to about 1.6 K from the table below 60 K; the test tolerates that. N2 at 37 K is 1.2 Pa (Pluto's air, about 1 Pa), where the old table gave 10 Pa. The rogue-planet nitrogen frost (`rogue_surface.nitrogen_vapor_pressure_pa`) and the water curve of `hydrosphere.py` still hold their own constants; moving them is a stored-value change for GEN.461 and the rogue-surface item.


### 6.71 Stand-ins of the resonance rings of small bodies and the ring gate near a star (GEN.1056)

`physics/small_body_rings.py` puts a narrow ring at the orbit that goes round `RESONANCE_ORDER` (3) times slower than a small body turns (Quaoar 1/3, Haumea 3:1, Chariklo near it; the design note's cases, 2ND-HAND), past the Roche limit of its ice and inside half its Hill radius, with a chance `RESONANCE_RING_RATE`; and refuses any ring inside `INNER_RING_LIMIT_AU`. The test points are the three known rings, never inputs: the resonance radius comes out within 5 percent of Quaoar's and Haumea's ring and 10 percent of Chariklo's (its mass is the least certain).

| fill_id | kind | tier | tweak | constant | knobs | validity | falsifier | replaced_by | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| RR1 | P | T4 | L3 | small_body_rings.RESONANCE_RING_RATE 0.10 and RESONANCE_ORDER 3 (the chance that a small body that meets the conditions has the resonance ring, and the order of the resonance; three of the roughly twenty bodies mapped by occultation have one, a selection-biased count) | rate 0.03 to 0.3; order 3 only | dwarf planets and centaurs under 5e22 kg | a survey of ring searches whose detection rate on bodies of this kind differs from 0.03 to 0.3, or a ring at another order | the survey statistic of the occultation ring searches (Morgado et al. 2023 and later) | test_small_body_rings::test_the_chance_is_the_registered_rate; test_small_body_rings::test_the_ring_orbits_three_times_slower_than_the_body_turns_where_the_known_rings_are |
| RR2 | P | T3 | L0 | small_body_rings.INNER_RING_LIMIT_AU 0.1 (no ring inside this distance from the star; Schlichting and Chang 2011 via the design note, 2ND-HAND, 'about 0.1 AU') | 0.05 to 0.3 | planets and small bodies round Sun-like stars | a ring measured round a planet inside 0.05 AU, or the lifetime calculation putting the limit outside 0.3 AU | the lifetime of a ring against the star's tides and heat, scaled with the star's luminosity | test_small_body_rings::test_no_ring_survives_inside_a_tenth_of_an_au |
| RR3 | P | T2 | L0 | small_body_rings.HILL_FRACTION_MAX 0.5 (the widest circular prograde orbit round a small body that stays bound, in Hill radii; Hamilton and Burns 1991, Domingos et al. 2006: 0.49) | 0.3 to 0.5 | prograde circular orbits | a stable prograde orbit measured outside 0.5 Hill radii | an N-body stability limit for the body's eccentricity | test_small_body_rings::test_a_resonance_outside_the_stable_zone_is_no_ring |
| RR4 | P | T6 | L3 | small_body_rings.RESONANCE_RING_MASS_KG (1e10, 1e14) and RESONANCE_WIDTH_FRACTION (0.002, 0.05) (the ring's mass and its width over its radius, log-uniform; the range gives a mean optical depth from 0.001 to 10 over a thin ring at a few thousand km) | mass one decade each way; width 0.001 to 0.1 | narrow rings of small bodies | an optical depth and a width measured for Quaoar's ring outside the range | the mass from the ring's optical depth and width as measured by occultations | test_small_body_rings::test_the_ring_is_thin_light_and_dense_enough_to_see |

### 6.73 Stand-ins of the disintegrating rocky planet (GEN.1041)

The wind that strips a rocky planet under about 0.1 Earth mass with a dayside over 2000 K (`physics/disintegrating_planet.py`): the vapour pressure of olivine, the isothermal Parker wind of the vapour and the full model's lower rate, the share of the time the planet loses mass, and the gate of the oddity. Source: Perez-Becker and Chiang 2013 (arXiv:1302.2147), read 2026-10-30 from a fetched copy whose abstract and sections 1 to 4.7 were verbatim and whose end (4.8 and the conclusions) was a summary; the equations and numbers below are those of the verbatim part. The paper writes the object KIC 12557548 b (KIC 1255b), the same as Kepler-1520 b. Kinds, tiers and tweak levels as in 6.1 and in the list of every stand-in. Not in it: the dust tail (the paper gives the dust loading as a ratio and no length), the transit depth (the observed 1.3 to 0.2 percent), and the optical depth of the dust in the surface temperature (eq. 6 with `tau` set to 0).

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| DP1 | D | T2 | L0 | disintegrating_planet.OLIVINE_MOLECULAR_MASS_H 169, OLIVINE_SUBLIMATION_ERG_G 3.21e10, OLIVINE_VAPOUR_CONSTANT_DYNE_CM2 6.72e14: the olivine vapour pressure of eq. 7 | olivine or pyroxene (60 m_H, 9.61e10, 3.13e11); iron is 50 times higher at 2145 K | a dayside of 1800 to 2400 K | a measured vapour pressure of the planet's real crust | the surface composition of the planet read from its stored bulk | Perez-Becker and Chiang 2013, eq. 7 and section 2.1 (read) |
| DP2 | D | T2 | L0 | disintegrating_planet.VAPOUR_MEAN_MASS_H 30 and ADIABATIC_INDEX 1.3: the vapour's mean mass and index (eq. 8); CORE_MASS_FRACTION 0.32 for the radius | gamma 1.0 halves the mass that survives 10 Gyr | silicate vapour | the paper's wind with its own radiative transfer | the vapour of the planet's own mixture | Perez-Becker and Chiang 2013, eq. 8 (read); the radius is the repository's own small-body relation |
| DP3 | T | T3 | L0 | disintegrating_planet.NON_ISOTHERMAL_FACTOR 1/40 and SOLID_ANGLE_SR 1: the full model's maximum rate over the isothermal one, and the wind's solid angle (eq. 27) | 1/40 (section 3.2: "about 40 times lower"); the factor depends on mass in the paper | planets of 0.01 to 0.1 Earth mass | the paper's own non-isothermal solution at other masses | a wind with radiative cooling | Perez-Becker and Chiang 2013, sections 3.2 and 4 (read) |

| DP5 | B | T3 | L0 | disintegrating_planet.DIVIDING_MASS_EARTH 0.11, MIN_DAYSIDE_TEMPERATURE_K 2000 and MAX_PERIOD_D 1.0: the gate of the oddity | 0.1 to 0.11 Earth mass; the full model's divide is about 0.08 | rocky bodies | a disintegrating planet over 0.11 Earth mass or under 2000 K | the module's own lifetime (a body whose lifetime is under its age) | the abstract and section 3.3 (read); the period gate is the design note's |

### 6.74 Stand-ins of atmosphere retention (GEN.355)

The library that answers when a body can hold an atmosphere (`physics/atmosphere_retention.py`): the shoreline ratio of the cumulative insolation over the fourth power of the escape velocity, the verdict and pressure cap it gives, and the Jeans gate on the gases. The exobase temperature, the cold-side vapour curves and the energy-limited rate are the thermosphere and saturation modules' and carry their own rows. Source: the design note (`atmospheres-retention-and-classes.md` section 3) from Zahnle and Catling 2017 (arXiv:1702.03386) and Catling and Kasting 2017, read second-hand; the 13 bodies are tested in `test_atmosphere_retention.py`. Kinds, tiers and tweak levels as in 6.1 and in the list of every stand-in.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

| SH2 | P | T6 | L2 | atmosphere_retention.VERDICT_EDGES (0.7, 1.0, 2.5, 30.0): the shoreline ratios under which a body holds a dense, moderate, tenuous or trace air and over which it holds none | none | a body of one star age | a body that holds air at a ratio over 30 | the logistic of GEN.511 | edges placed so the 13 bodies fall on the observed side: Earth, Venus, Titan and Triton dense, Pluto moderate, Mars, Io and Ganymede tenuous, Callisto and Europa trace, Mercury, the Moon and Ceres none |
| SH3 | P | T7 | L3 | atmosphere_retention.PRESSURE_CAPS_PA (none, 1e4, 1e3, 1, 0): the surface pressure each verdict allows, 0.1 bar, 10 mbar, 10 micro-bar | none | pressure caps of a body of one star | a measured pressure over its cap | the supply-limited pressure of the pressure stage | design: Mars holds 6 mbar at a ratio of 1.8 and Pluto 10 micro-bar at 0.78 (the pressure is capped, supply and vapour set the rest) |

### 6.75 Stand-ins of the nightside collapse of a locked planet's air (GEN.1044)

`physics/nightside_collapse.py` says whether the air of a tidally locked rocky planet freezes onto its night side and how much stays: the CO2 critical pressure is the empirical fit of Wordsworth 2015 (0.14 bar at Earth's flux for an Earth-mass planet, read in the paper, Eq. 43 and Table 2), the night side's temperature under a thin air is the paper's radiative estimate (Eq. 10 and 25), and the gas left is the vapour pressure there. Other gases use the same balance with no calibration. The Moon, Mars and the Earth are tests, not inputs. Design: `docs/design/composition/nightside-collapse-model.md`. Lock-free; the wiring is GEN.1051.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

| NC3 | P | T1 | L0 | nightside_collapse.KAPPA_M2_KG 5e-5 and COS_ALPHA 0.5 (the grey optical depth of the thin-air night side) | none | CO2 air under about 1 bar | the paper's 125.2 K at 0.1 bar, Earth's flux, albedo 0.2 (our Eq. 25 gives 126.9 K) | a correlated-k night-side temperature | Wordsworth 2015 Eq. 10, 25 and 27, READ; test_nightside_collapse::test_the_night_side_of_a_tenth_of_a_bar_is_the_paper_s_125_k |

### 6.76 Stand-ins of the super-puff radius (GEN.1042)

The gate, envelope range, occurrence and tidal inflation of a super-puff (`physics/super_puff.py`). The radius itself is not a stand-in: it is the envelope law of Lopez and Fortney 2014 (`bulk_structure`) inside its range and the giant tables past it. Sources read on 2026-10-30: Lee and Chiang 2016 (arXiv:1510.08855, body verbatim), Millholland 2019 (arXiv:1910.06794, body verbatim, equations from plain-text extraction), Libby-Roberts et al. 2025 (arXiv:2505.21358, abstract and sections to 4.1 verbatim) for Kepler-51 d, Piaulet et al. 2021 (arXiv:2011.13444, to section 6.1 verbatim) for WASP-107 b. Not found in any of them: a fading law of the radius with age (the fade is the envelope law's age term plus the XUV loss of `envelope_escape`), the masses and radii of Kepler-51 b and c (Masuda et al. 2024 was not fetched; b is from Lee and Chiang's quote of Masuda 2014), WASP-107 b in Millholland, and any occurrence statistic.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| PF1 | B | T4 | L0 | super_puff.MAX_AGE_GY 1, with MAX_MASS_EARTH 10, PERIOD_RANGE_D (10, 100) and DENSITY_RANGE_G_CM3 (0.03, 0.1): the gate | Lee and Chiang's class is 2 to 6 Earth masses and under 50 days; the design table is 10 Earth masses and 10 to 100 days | rocky and gas-dwarf planets with an envelope | a super-puff older than 1 Gyr or heavier than 10 Earth masses | the age-dependent radius of a measured sample | the design table (weird-worlds.md W15), Lee and Chiang 2016 Introduction and Sec. 4 (read), Kepler-51 d 0.038 g/cc (read) |

### 6.77 Stand-ins of the helium enrichment of a thinning envelope (GEN.1045)

The step `envelope_escape` lacks: it follows the mass of a hydrogen-helium envelope and keeps it solar, so no air it leaves is helium-rich (audit of 2026-10-30; `physics/helium_fractionation.py`). Sources read on 2026-10-30: Hu, Seager and Yung 2015 (arXiv:1505.02221, equations 9 to 15 and Sec. 2.1, body verbatim, equations from plain-text extraction) and Malsky, Rogers, Kempton and Marounina (arXiv:2309.16054, appendix A, body verbatim; the fetched text shows no journal or year). The crossover mass is in neither paper; the diffusion-limited flux replaces it. Not found: a closed form for the ion-neutral collision frequency in b' (Schunk and Nagy 1980 is cited, not read), the transonic reduction factor `f_r`, and a helium mass fraction as a function of loss share. The module takes the total loss from `envelope_escape` and does not change it, so what it gives is the composition only; the design's validity bound (hydrogen mass fraction under 1e-3) is not applied.

| fill_id | kind | tier | tweak | constant | knobs | validity | falsifier | replaced_by | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| HF1 | D | T2 | L0 | helium_fractionation.DIFFUSION_A_CM 1.04e18 and DIFFUSION_EXPONENT 0.732: the binary diffusion coefficient of hydrogen in helium | none (both papers quote Mason and Marrero 1970) | 3,000 to 10,000 K | a measured diffusion coefficient at the homopause | the table of Mason and Marrero 1970 | Hu et al. 2015 eq. 9, Malsky et al. 2023 A3 (read) |
| HF2 | T | T3 | L1 | helium_fractionation.HOMOPAUSE_TEMPERATURE_K 1e4, IONISED_SHARE 0.1 and HOMOPAUSE_RADIUS_OVER_TRANSIT 1.1: the homopause; and b' = b / (1 + x), which gives the paper's 8.0e20 cm^-1 s^-1 at 1e4 K and x = 0.1 | T 3,000 to 10,000 K (the weaker fractionation is at the higher T); x 0 to 0.5 | planets of 4 to 20 Earth masses with a thin envelope | the ion-neutral frequency of Schunk and Nagy 1980 in the full b' | b' from the ionisation fraction at the homopause at each step (Malsky et al. 2023 A3) | Hu et al. 2015 Sec. 2.1; Malsky et al. 2023 A.1, A.4 (read); the form 1/(1 + x) is a fit to the one value of Hu |
| HF3 | B | T3 | L0 | helium_fractionation.SOLAR_ENVELOPE_HELIUM 0.245 and MIN_HELIUM_FOR_ENHANCED 0.4: the starting helium share and the line of the enhanced planets | start 0.24 to 0.27 | hydrogen-helium envelopes | a measured primordial envelope helium | the solar mix of the star's own composition | Malsky et al. 2023 Introduction and A (X 0.74, Y 0.24) (read) |

### 6.78 Stand-ins of the giant-impact tilt of an ice giant (GEN.1053)

The tilt an impact gives an ice giant, the planes that follow it, and the rate (`physics/giant_tilt.py`). Sources read on 2026-10-30: Kegerreis et al. 2018 (arXiv:1803.07083, full text verbatim: impactors of 1, 2, 3 Earth masses on 14.536 minus the impactor, speed at infinity 5 km/s, angular momentum 1 to 10e36 kg m^2/s, no pre-impact spin, atmosphere at least 90 percent kept, debris clouds "quite spherical") and Morbidelli et al. 2012 (arXiv:1208.4685, full text verbatim: impulsive tilt, at least two giant collisions, an earlier obliquity of 4 to 8 degrees or more for prograde moons, quoting Slattery et al. 1992 for an equatorial debris disk of 1 to 3 percent of the mass). Not found: the final obliquity of any run (Kegerreis computes the spin, not the obliquity), a distribution of impactor masses or of the impact parameter, and a rate for tilts over 60 degrees; the "one in five" is the design table's count of the Solar System. Slattery et al. 1992 itself was not read.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

### 6.79 Stand-ins of the Hycean box (GEN.963)

The four ranges of a Hycean candidate (`physics/hycean_candidate.py`), a flag derived from mass, radius, equilibrium temperature and density on the classes T, I and V. The ranges are the item's (GEN.963, after Madhusudhan et al. 2021); that paper was not read in this build, so the ranges are a quoted definition and not a verified one. The hydrosphere state `hycean` (`hydrosphere.py`, a liquid surface ocean under air over half hydrogen) and the envelope survival (`envelope_escape`, GEN.822; `atmosphere_retention`, GEN.355) are not changed; the flag only names the planets the box selects.

No source was opened for this module: the design's own [R] numbers (the chaotic zone of Wisdom 1980, the minimum-mass nebula, Malhotra resonance sweeping, Safronov ejection) are recalled, and every share is a stand-in, as the design says (tier educated guess from physics, L1 to L3, one registered row each). ### 6.102 Stand-ins of the smoothed rogue density (GEN.1115)

The smoothing of the star density a rogue planet is drawn from (`galaxy/rogue_density.py`; design `rogue-planet-drift.md` sections 3.3 and 5). The widths are the rms offsets `rogue_drift.drift` gives for the design's stand-in kick distribution (median 3 km/s, ln-sigma 0.8, isotropic, 1 to 10 Gyr), so they are only as good as that distribution (GEN.1113 replaces it); the design note's first guess of 0.5 kpc radially is replaced by the computed 190 pc (the note's own range is 0.1 to 0.7 kpc). ### 6.104 Stand-ins of the dwarf planet population (GEN.1090)

The dwarf planets of a belt or outer disc (`physics/dwarf_population.py`; design `belt-origins.md` section 5). The round masses are rows BR9, the clearing numbers rows BR6 and BR7 (section 6.95), whose constants are named there now. The dwarf planet count is not typed in: it follows from the belt's mass and size law (rows BR19 and BR20), and the main belt's 200 to 250 bodies of 100 km and the Kuiper belt's 15 to 20 dwarf candidates are test targets only. One new row.

| fill_id | kind | tier | tweak | constant | knobs | validity | falsifier | replaced_by | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| DPM1 | P | T6 | L1 | dwarf_population.ICE_BODY_FRACTION 0.5: the ice mass fraction at and above which a body is as weak as pure ice; the round mass runs log-linearly from the rock value at no ice to the ice value here | 0.3 to 1.0 | belt members and dwarf planets | a body of 20 to 40 percent ice whose measured shape is far from the mixed round mass (Ceres, round at 9.4e20 kg and about a quarter water, sits inside it) | the relaxed hydrostatic mass from the body's strength and heat (BR9) | educated guess from physics (a mixture is as weak as its weak phase once that phase is half the mass) |
